# Review log: Name who starts the next session's runtime on a recycled pod

## Standing context

Changelog (compaction pass of 2026-10-01, after non-spec round 2). This pass lifted the durable residue of every Ledger entry from redesign1.apply.1 through non-spec.2.review-test-coverage.1, and of the three entries filed under `## Retired` (redesign1.apply.3 to redesign1.apply.5). It added the Settled staging and tree-fact entries, the Traps of spec rounds 1 to 20 and non-spec rounds 1 and 2, the carried non-spec Open lines, and four Deferred entries. It deleted the closed Open entries (sidecar `preConnect`, closed by f1.human-decisions; TEST-11 timing, closed by non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1) and the closed Deferred entry on `execution-modes.md:59` (closed by spec.4.fix-G2.1). It merged the decision-8 cap Traps and the fallback-drain Trap into the decision-11 Trap, because their replacement was itself replaced. It recorded two disagreements as UNVERIFIED (the `USER_REVOKED` disposition and the `sessions_served` source). It did not reach the 200-line target: the section is about 300 lines, because the blast-radius table (about 75 lines) and the Traps carry content later rounds cite. `## Ledger` and `## Retired` were not edited.

### Settled

Decisions:

- **Runtime lifetime (decision 1).** The runtime process lives as long as the pod on every pool and in both deployment models (human decision, 2026-09-30). No close at occupancy zero, no retirement keyed on deployment model, and no runtime redial.
- **Unconditional adapter rule (D2).** The adapter reads no recycle, acknowledgment, or cross-tenant setting, because the recycle `Shutdown` follows the last slot's close on the concurrent path; every non-reuse case is a gateway retire or a pool-admission refusal (D5).
- **Admission rule (decision 2, D5, CODE-11).** Process reuse requires the existing `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation`, enforced at pool admission for a recycling pool with `maxSessionsPerPod > 1` outside `vm-restart`. `vm-restart` is exempt because `Decide` retires such a pod at every boundary. No boundary gate and no `process_reuse_unacknowledged` reason exist.
- **No drain of a pod pinned to another tenant (decision 11).** No acquisition drains a pod it refuses because the pod is pinned to another tenant, on any pool (human decision, 2026-10-01, superseding decision 8's cap and the earlier D6 drain). Archived Ledger entries that stage, cap, or test `CrossTenantDrain`, `drainOneRefused`, or `kept_runtime_tenant_change` describe the withdrawn staging. A finding that restores any acquisition-time drain of a pod pinned to another tenant, or caps the D16 drain, re-opens the decision.
- **Fallback pin read (decision 9).** The Postgres fallback reads the pin inside `ClaimIdle`'s row-locked transaction, `ClaimIdle` excludes refused rows, `AdmitTenantPin` writes nothing, every drain stamp is written through `DrainRefusedPod` from the idle scan (no reason parameter, no boolean result since decision 11, no error result since decision 12), and the fallback writes no drain stamp (D16). A finding that moves the pin read out of the transaction, writes under the row lock, or filters on the mirror's `tenant_id` re-opens the decision.
- **Hold-timeout no-frame test (decision 10).** TEST-3 asserts that the coordinator hold timeout writes no `terminate` frame.
- **Boundary liveness (D4).** A runtime that has stopped at a boundary retires the pod through the adapter's `runtime_live` report. The fail-fast `Start` covers only a runtime that stops after the report. `ended` is sticky.
- **Generation (D9).** The generation never resets inside the pod's life, and `soleSession` requires its session to be live.
- **Retire polarity (D13).** The new `Decide` input `RuntimeNotLive` is true to retire; the wire field `runtime_live` is positive and an omitted value reads false.
- **No `terminate` frame (decision 5, D8).** No path sends the `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` `terminate` frame, at a session's teardown or at pod exit. The pod-exit drain belongs to remediation-plan steps R16 and R17. A finding that asks for a pod-exit frame, an edge out of `READY` in §15.4.2, or a `DrainRuntimeForPodExit` re-opens the decision.
- **Hold-timeout teardown (D10, CODE-4, SPEC-18).** The coordinator hold timeout ends the sidecar runtime through the pod-scope teardown, before pass 1 and after `hold.mu` is released. It is a pod-scope event, so it does not reopen D2 or D3. Retirement after it uses the `runtime_not_live`, failed-start, and orphan-claim paths. SPEC-18 deletes the §10.1.4 `terminate` clause and states the close (SPEC-18(b)); SPEC-2 names it in one clause. Adapter exit at the timeout and a gateway `AdapterTerminating` consumer are pre-existing gaps.
- **Pinned idle inventory (decision of 2026-09-30, option D; D14).** It is the interim rule, and SPEC-8(c) states it. A finding that asks for part of the follow-up's scope (spec-changes §9.1, **Pinned idle bound and idle timeout**) re-opens the decision.
- **Pinned-idle bound inputs (D14).** On an admin-API pool `maxWarm` equals `warmCount`, and `minWarm` drops below it only through observed demand, a `bootstrapMinWarm` override, an active `scalePolicy.scaleToZero` window, a paused experiment, or an active experiment variant's `initialMinWarm`; otherwise the bound (`maxWarm` minus the target) is zero. D14 names all five inputs (non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1, correcting "zero until demand is observed").
- **Outside-rule pools fail closed (decision 6, D16).** An admitted pool edit that takes a pool outside the process-reuse rule ends reuse at the next acquisition. Every acquisition, including the reserved-hold rebind and the Postgres fallback, refuses a pod that has served a session, ends a `reserved` hold it reads, and stamps a refused idle pod for drain (uncapped) when the idle scan reads it `idle`. Archived Ledger entries that let a `reserved` or pinned idle pod serve one more session in its kept process, that bar a drain tied to a pool edit, or that word that session's `residualStateWarning` describe the withdrawn staging. A finding that restores the transition session re-opens the decision.
- **D16 stamp guard.** The gateway stamps `lenny.dev/drain-request` only after a direct `Get` of `claim-<name>` returns NotFound, and only at an acquisition that reads the pod `idle` (f1.human-decisions, redesign OD-2).
- **Failed D16 stamp (decision 12).** `DrainRefusedPod` returns no value, a failed stamp does not fail the acquisition (non-spec §4.4), and the Postgres fallback writes no drain stamp (D16). A finding that fails an acquisition on a failed stamp or adds a metric for it re-opens the decision (spec-changes §12, decision 12).
- **No `sdks/` edit (decisions 3 and 7).** The Go SDK's doc comments and the SDK behaviour they describe belong to the follow-up runtime-SDK proposal, including the §15.7 `CreateRequest` `SessionID` and `TaskID` comments and `Handler.OnTerminate`. No checklist step depends on it, on the condition that it lands before any release. A finding that asks for an SDK comment or code edit here, an SDK guard, or a checklist dependency on the follow-up re-opens the decision.
- **Step order (decision 3).** The pin and admission step (CODE-8, CODE-10, CODE-11) lands before the transport step (CODE-1 to CODE-4), so no landed tree keeps a runtime process without the pin check and the admission rule (reconcile.checklist.3).
- **"Runtime not live" (D15).** The new retire condition has one statement, the §5.2 Pod retirement policy item "Runtime not live". Every other site names the item by its label, and TEST-16 fails on a restatement.
- **Rule homes.** Runtime lifetime: SPEC-2. Hold-timeout close: SPEC-18(b). "Runtime not live" and its terminal retire: SPEC-7(a). Acknowledgment, outside-rule hold end, refusal, and D16 drain: SPEC-8(d). Pin persistence, the refused pod left pinned, and pinned idle inventory: SPEC-8(c). Scrub reach: SPEC-6(b), with step 1b's reach in step 1b. Interrupt degradation: the §15.4.3 `Interrupt` row (SPEC-11(c)). Heartbeat escalation: SPEC-14(a).
- **Proposal format.** D-entries, the §3 overview, and the §5 edge-case table restate staged text by design and are not single-source copies (spec.2.review-single-source.1).
- **Checklist pointers.** Checklist step lines name deliverable ids, every id written out, plus the owning deliverables' heading names (reconcile.checklist.1).
- **Step names (decision 13, Option A).** Prose outside the implementation checklist names a step by the deliverables it lands. Step numbers appear only in the checklist's own lines, including Depends-on and the register-writers notes. A finding that a summary, spec-changes, or non-spec-changes sentence cites a wrong step number is closed by naming the deliverables.
- **Adjudicated decisions.** Decisions 1 to 5 and redesign decisions A to C (2026-09-30), decisions 6 to 11 and the step ordering in decision 3 (2026-10-01), decision 12 (2026-10-02), and decision 13 (f4.human-decisions.13) are adjudicated. A finding that argues for a rejected alternative re-opens a human decision.
- **Decisions A to C.** CODE-7 logs every `released` retire at `Info` with `pod_id` and `reason` (A); SPEC-5(b) names "Runtime not live" (B); DOC-6 is its own deliverable (C).
- **Sidecar SDK-warm.** No sidecar transport implements `SDKWarmRuntime`; the gap predates this proposal and is filed separately (§9.1 bullet, summary defect entry). Restricting `preConnect` to the embedded model is not staged (f1.human-decisions).
- **Superseded Ledger entries.** A withdrawn entry is superseded through the standing context and never deleted from the Ledger (f1.human-decisions).
- **SPEC-4(h) placement.** SPEC-4(h) inserts its delete mid-row in the §4.6.3 ownership row, so the gateway's deletes stay together (f1.human-decisions).
- **SPEC-12(g) placement.** The hold-ending `DELETE` stays inside §29.2 step 7's reserved branch; a separate step cannot take a letter suffix (f1.human-decisions).
- **`WarmPoolOversized`.** Filed separately by a §9.1 non-goal; no evaluator runs the rule and `lenny_pool_min_warm` has no emitter (non-spec.1.fix-G6.1).
- **Shipped-tree defects.** The summary's defects section carries the §16.1 retirement-counter row, first-session manifest ordering, scrub steps 3 and 5 and the `preConnect` re-warm, embedded recycling wiring, claim-path defects, adapter signals, and sidecar SDK-warm.
- **Impacts.** Rows exist for 0084, 0071, 0080, 0073, and 0087; the 0078 row was refuted because 0078 stages no spec edit. Summary text on 0078 and 0084 outside the impacts section stays (f3.cleanup).

Staging:

- **§4.7.9 later session (SPEC-1).** A later session on a sidecar pod skips §4.7.9 steps 7, 8, and 9 and uses the connections the runtime opened on the first session.
- **§29.2 trace (SPEC-12(a)).** The `CH-MSGSOCK` dial is traced at step 25 for the first session of a sidecar pod-warm pod; step 29 is an `adapter`, `internal` step that states both models; steps 26a to 28 open "On the first session of a pod-warm pod".
- **§15.4.1 (SPEC-11(d)).** A pure deletion of "the runtime knows it's receiving its first message by virtue of just having started"; SPEC-5(b) ("keyed by the frame's `sessionId`") is the home for telling sessions apart.
- **§15.4.2 (SPEC-11(b)).** The session-end edge is `ACTIVE` → `READY` "(session ends; the pod is recycled)", "exit on completion" is deleted, and no edge leaves `READY`.
- **SDK process (SPEC-2, SPEC-9(g)).** §6.1's "agent process" is the SDK process that `DemoteSDK` tears down; neither it nor re-warm ends the runtime process. SPEC-9(g) replaces "The agent process is NOT started." with "No session is bound, and no message for the next session has been delivered."
- **Client disclosure (SPEC-8(e), SPEC-10, D12).** `podReuse` and `residualStateWarning` are pool-configuration signals ("a pool configured to reuse pods") and over-warn on pools whose pods serve one session.
- **Idle accounting (SPEC-8(c), SPEC-16, SPEC-17, CODE-10).** `ReadyCount`, the `lenny_warmpool_idle_pods` gauge, `idlePodCount`, and `PoolWarmingUp` exclude pinned idle pods; the idle pod-minutes counter observes `ReadyCount + PinnedIdleCount`; pinned drains go in `DrainPinned`; CODE-10 confirms a cached claim NotFound with an uncached `APIReader` `Get` before it sets `Pinned`.
- **§4.6.1 pin sentence (SPEC-4(g)).** Replaced by a pointer to §5.2 that carries no rule content; its locator names §4.6.1 **Reserved hold (claim retention across same-tenant sessions):**.
- **§13.1 (SPEC-15).** Credential claims are qualified by pointers to the SPEC-8(d) label, and the guidance for a recycling pool names `maxSessionsPerPod: 1` or `vm-restart`.
- **Hold-end sites.** SPEC-4(h), SPEC-9(j), SPEC-9(k), SPEC-12(g), SPEC-14(g), and SPEC-16 name the acquisition-ended hold wherever the spec stated a TTL-only hold end; the remaining "hold expiry" sites are non-exclusive.
- **§29.10 (SPEC-12(h)).** Deletes "Nothing in this subsection applies to a pod serving one session at a time ..."; the intra-pod MCP item stays.
- **§5.2 webhook item 2 (SPEC-8(c)).** Deletes "(pod return to pool)"; the pinned-idle exit list ends "or when an acquisition drains it".
- **SPEC-8(f).** `allowCrossTenantReuse` "applies only to" the sequential-reuse path; admission requires microvm isolation, a `vm-restart` or `in-place` scrub profile, `maxConcurrentSessions` 1, and a non-T4 runtime, and does not require `recycle.enabled` (spec.18.fix-G1.1).
- **Step 1b (SPEC-6(a)).** Reads "Purge the `shmget`-allocated IPC shared memory segments the adapter's UID owns or created"; the best-effort parenthetical stays as a bare pointer.
- **Non-spec homes.** Non-spec §4.4 owns refusal handling and CODE-8 cites it; non-spec §4.5 cites D6 for the planner exclusion; decision 12 edits non-spec §4.4 and §6, CODE-8, TEST-12, TEST-13, and TEST-21, and SPEC-8(d) states its rule.
- **Description rewrites.** CODE-11 rewrites the OpenAPI `allowCrossTenantReuse` and `scrubProfile` descriptions (regenerated into `pkg/ops/mcp/generated_tools.go`); CODE-9 rewrites the `runtimestore.go` field comment and the three "until the claim-hold TTL expires" comments (`catalog.go`, `reserved_meter.go`, `plan.go`).
- **DOC-2 glossary.** Deletes the retire-trigger sentence at `docs/reference/glossary.md:302` and edits "Recycling requires no runtime cooperation and works at every integration level." as DOC-3 states.
- **DOC-6 sites.** DOC-6 stages `docs/api/internal.md:447` step 6, `state-machines.md:89` and `:108`, `concepts.md:37`, `:63`, `:67`, `:79`, and step 3 of `:97`, and `architecture.md:65`.
- **TEST-16 dispositions.** `TestVMRestartDocsStateRetireAndReprovision_F5232` re-anchors on "Under the `vm-restart` scrub profile" and drops "reuses the continuing microvm guest"; `TestAdapterContractNamesTheShutdownRPCUnderItsWireName` drops "no other bound session".
- **TEST-14.** Asserts "globex binds another pod" and "no SandboxClaim names the acme-pinned pod", because the Kind install runs the tenant-label webhook.
- **TEST-20.** A `CloseListener` hook that records `s.slotCount()` equal to the member count pins CODE-4's teardown before pass 1.
- **TEST-21.** The tier-9 second subtest reaches `minWarm` 0 through one `PUT /v1/admin/pools/{name}` with `bootstrapMinWarm: 0` while A's pod is `reserved`; one tier-2 case pins both `endReservedHolds` skips; the tier-11 bullet updates `claimOwnershipRowSentence`, `claimWriterSetCell`, and the mutation case, and the register-writers case stays red from the SPEC-4 step until the pin step.
- **TEST-11.** Creates session B inside the reserved hold (default `gateway.claimHoldTTLSeconds` 10), so the zero pinned-idle bound never applies to it.

Tree facts:

- **Restart policy.** Agent pods render `RestartPolicy: Never` (`podspec.go:980`), so no container restarts and no runtime redials; §4.7.11's `CrashLoopBackOff` is a pre-existing spec outlier.
- **Pod-exit signals.** The runtime container has no preStop hook and gets the kubelet SIGTERM at once (grace `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`, default 120 s); the adapter cannot signal the sidecar runtime and writes no `shutdown` frame; the adapter's SIGTERM goroutine already closes `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` before the eviction checkpoint (pre-existing).
- **Heartbeat.** The monitor runs per Attach stream; `onHeartbeatHung` calls `rt.Interrupt`, a no-op under D3, and the stream ends.
- **Handshake and MCP.** `RuntimeOps.Run` handshakes once per accepted connection; the embedded path opens neither `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` nor intra-pod MCP; cancelling the pod MCP surface closes only the listener, so accepted connections keep serving.
- **Gateway reads.** The gateway cluster client is uncached (100 QPS, burst 200 per replica); the gateway SA has `get`/`patch` on Pods and no `list`/`watch`, so `AdmitTenantPin` is one direct GET per candidate (about 1 + P/(U+1) per acquisition), and a Pod LIST remedy needs a §4.6.3 RBAC edit.
- **Controller reads.** The WarmPoolController `APIReader` is rate-limited by the shared "other" bucket (10 QPS, burst 100) with one default worker; the pool reconciler owns Sandboxes, watches SandboxWarmPool, Sandbox, and PDB only, requeues every 60 s, and already holds `get`/`list`/`watch` on Pods and SandboxClaims.
- **Tenant-label webhook.** Admits the WarmPoolController SA's `{tenant_id} → unassigned` on any pool and refuses `unassigned → {tenant_id}` and `{A} → {B}`; `Claimer.Claim` creates the claim and writes `bound` before `stampPodTenant` and returns a stamp error without deleting the claim, so a visible pin implies a visible claim; tenant IDs are never empty.
- **Drain consumer.** `reconcileDrainRequest` drains from any phase (`requireIdle=false`), and a `draining` Sandbox keeps its in-flight sessions; `StampDrainRequest` writes an RFC3339Nano value, so each re-stamp is an etcd write, and returns nil on NotFound.
- **Occupancy projection.** A claimless `reserved` pod projects `idle` and a claimless `claimed` pod `draining`; pinned idle pods come only from `reserved → idle`; a claim deleted before the first bound patch leaves an `idle` pod untouched.
- **`Decide` order.** Fail-policy, scrub-failure limit, `VMRestart`, session count, uptime, host schedulability; `RuntimeNotLive` sits after uptime and before host; `VMRestart` derives from `scrubProfile` alone.
- **Boundary re-read.** `InspectForRecycle` re-reads the pool record at every scrub report; a disabled recycle returns no policy and the pod retires on the session path; a missing pool row yields no recycle.
- **`KeepsRuntime` source.** Resolved from the pool store; no row resolves false; a read error fails resolution, so a Postgres failover cannot mass-drain pinned pods.
- **Isolation level.** `claimAtCreate` computes `sessionIsolationLevel` from the same `PoolMatch` it claims with, and the create paths persist it; `KeepsRuntime` true implies a non-empty `scrubPolicy`.
- **Recycle counters.** `sessions_served` and `scrub_failure_count` live in Postgres `agent_pod_state`; `maxSessionsPerPod` is required with no default; `acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation` is accepted on `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` pools.
- **Coordinator hold.** Pod-scoped; arms only when a session has started; fence and timer are exclusive under `hold.mu`; operational RPCs are refused while held; `onHoldTimeout` does not exit the adapter, and the gateway has no `AdapterTerminating` handler.
- **Slot health.** `slothealth.Tracker` is in-memory per gateway replica, and its only production `RecordFailure` caller is the bind-failure path.
- **Scrub UIDs.** Step 1 runs `kill -9 -1` as the adapter UID (`SandboxUser` empty) and spares the adapter because it is PID 1; the adapter (65532) and agent (65533) UIDs differ; `/tmp` and `/dev/shm` are emptyDir volumes in both containers; a non-root `ipcrm` cannot remove the runtime UID's segments.
- **SDK-warm.** Only the embedded `SDKWarmInProcessRuntime` implements `SDKWarmRuntime`; `ConfigureWorkspace` and `DemoteSDK` return Unimplemented otherwise, so every sidecar start is `StartSession`.
- **Pool inventory.** The planner target is `min(minWarm, maxWarm)` and the excess drain sheds unpinned idle pods to it, so total idle stays at or below `maxWarm`, which §17 `pods_floor` sums; `PoolWarmingUp` requires `minWarm > 0`; `idlePodCount` and `PoolWarmingUp` derive from `status.readyCount`; Sandboxes carry a controller ownerReference, so the §10.7 delete at `readyCount <= 0` cascades.
- **Admin-API warm counts.** The admin API has no `maxWarm` field; the PoolScalingController rewrites both spec fields every sync; the pool-config validator denies non-PSC spec writes; a PUT never clears `bootstrapMinWarm` (only `DELETE .../bootstrap-override` does); convergence needs 48 h of data.
- **Postgres fallback.** Runs after `podClaimQueueTimeout` with the apiserver reachable and still creates the SandboxClaim; `claimSelectSQL` is `ORDER BY updated_at LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`; the mirror writes `tenant_id` empty for idle rows; `Binder.connect` runs the fallback after every primary `ErrNoIdlePod`; the claim queue re-enters every 250 ms.
- **Reserved holds.** `rebindReserved` is tenant-scoped; `endReservedHolds` ends every `reserved` hold it reads regardless of tenant; `DeleteOnHoldExpiry` checks only UID and resourceVersion, so the "claim not in reserved" skip is the only guard against deleting a rebound claim.
- **Interrupt.** The only gateway caller of the adapter `Interrupt` RPC is the client interrupt endpoint; the no-op `Interrupt` repairs §7.2 path 4, which writes after the interrupt.
- **Direct-mode usage.** Token folds resolve through `soleSession`, so a kept process's later direct-mode sessions report zero deltas (`zero_delta`); decision 1 owns it; the gateway has no `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS` consumer.
- **Kind harness.** The Kind install runs the tenant-label webhook; `bootstrap-overlay.gen.yaml` is gitignored and generated at install; `lenny-sandboxclaim-guard` runs on CREATE only.
- **Gates.** No gate resolves quoted bold labels (`scripts/specshift/gate` checks line citations only); tier-11 `requireLine` and `lineContaining` return the first matching line; `nonceStatementBlock` matches an anchor's first line per physical line; `spec_28_register_writers_test.go` pins the §4.6.3 SandboxClaim row and the §28.3 `REG-CLAIM` cell byte-exact.
- **Proposal 0078.** Stages no spec edit, so it moves no 0079 anchor.

#### Per-session-process blast radius

Rule. A sentence that assumes a per-session runtime process or an adapter signal path to the runtime is staged when (a) it is the home of a fact this proposal decides (who starts the runtime, §4.7.9 step 7; the runtime's lifetime, §4.7.10), (b) this proposal changes its truth (what a session end, interrupt, missed heartbeat, or occupancy zero does to the runtime; when `terminate` is sent), or (c) it restates a staged sentence in docs, a schema description, or a code comment. Otherwise it was already false before this proposal for a reason this proposal does not change, and it keeps its wording under a named spec-changes §9.1 class: P1 (the adapter signals the sidecar runtime or writes `shutdown`), P2 (the adapter reads the runtime's exit code or stderr), P3 (first-session start-up ordering), or OD3 (spec-changes §12, decision 3). T means true after this proposal. Staged text never restates a P-class claim. This table supersedes the per-round WATCHOUT, OPEN, and UNVERIFIED entries on these sites. File a site not in this table only after classifying it by this rule.

Ground truth for classes P1 and P2 is the summary's defect entry on adapter signals (summary.md, Defects in the shipped tree). `terminate` has one production writer in the shipped tree (pkg/adapter/session.go:512-520, reached only from `tearDownReclaimedSlot`) and none after this proposal (D8).

### Traps

Withdrawn designs (each stays while the design that replaced it stands):

- **Reverted facet rounds.** Commits 8cdd5d6d through ccaeb30d (reverted by 39e08bb4) re-opened each facet one round at a time. Check any new finding against D3, D4, D8, and D9 before editing.
- **Successor-runtime label.** The `lenny.dev/runtime-recreatable` pod label and the `no_successor_runtime` retire (reconciler stamp, gateway parse, `Inputs.RuntimeRecreatable`) are deleted. They retired every sidecar recycling pod at occupancy zero, and the 2026-09-30 decision keeps the runtime process instead. Decision 2 took Alternative B, so the label does not return.
- **Successor-runtime mechanisms.** A restartable runtime container, an adapter-side spawn generalising `SpawnPath`, and a platform supervisor as the runtime container's PID 1 are deleted from §9. A kept process needs no successor, so a finding that proposes one re-derives a rejected design.
- **Adapter-side conditional close.** It lost to the unconditional rule (D2). On the concurrent path the recycle `Shutdown` arrives after the last slot's `Close` (`answerShutdown`), so the adapter cannot see the disposition when it would close. Making `Interrupt` close the connection on the sole session re-derives it and would EOF every sibling on a concurrent pod.
- **Redial and reader-stopped reset.** Clearing the reader-stopped flag on a new accept, and any runtime redial, are deleted. No second accept exists once the connection is kept, and redial was a facet of the reverted attempt (commit 68fb2a01). `ended` is sticky (D4).
- **Hung-runtime flag.** A heartbeat-hung flag feeding `ServesNextSession` was rejected: a sequential pod already retires on the failed session and a concurrent pod on the slot-retry trigger, and a flag would retire a pod after one slow ack.
- **Positive `RuntimeLive` input.** It lost to retire polarity (D13), because every existing reuse literal (`podscrub_test.go` `base()`, the tier-4 recycle literal) would flip to retire.
- **Retire branch placement.** Placing the new branch before the session-count branch (the earlier label branch's `VMRestart` reason-stability argument) lost to placing it after uptime (non-spec §4.3). Moving it earlier changes the counted reason of every `maxSessionsPerPod: 1` pool. The edge row "the `VMRestart` branch runs first" is relative to the new branch only.
- **Old TEST-8.** The tier-7a concurrent retire-once case is deleted. It exercised `SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot`, which this proposal does not change; TEST-15 extends `vm_restart_recycle_disposition` instead.
- **Separate §6.2 drain edge.** A separate `claimed ──→ draining` edge lost to clauses on the one recycle edge (SPEC-9(c)), because `vm-restart` is already a clause inside that edge.
- **§16.1 retirement-counter row.** The old row edit is dropped; the row's omission of non-counting reasons predates this proposal and is filed separately (§9.1). SPEC-16 returned `spec/16` to files touched for the idle-pod and reserved-pods rows only. The §5.2 sentence "When the gateway retires a pod it increments ..." belongs to the same class, `host_unschedulable` and `scrub_report_timeout` appear nowhere in spec/, and D7's "freezes" wording is imprecise but unedited.
- **Restated retire predicate.** Restating the new condition at each `vm-restart` carve-out lost to one statement (D15), because the copies drifted each round. A site names the condition by its label.
- **Retirement audit record.** It lost to the CODE-7 `Info` log, because no code writes such a record (`claimDispositionDriver.Retire` logs only a `failed` retire).
- **Boundary acknowledgment gate.** `process_reuse_unacknowledged`, `Inputs.ProcessReuseUnacknowledged`, `PodRecyclePolicy.ProcessReuseUnacknowledged`, and the §5.2 item "Process reuse not acknowledged" lost to the CODE-11 admission rule (decision 2). The gate admitted a pool whose every pod then retired after one session and needed a restatement at every `vm-restart` carve-out.
- **Pod-exit `terminate` frame.** `Server.DrainRuntimeForPodExit`, its SIGTERM call, `drainViaLifecycle`, `drainReason`, TEST-17, the `eviction` reason home in the §4.7 `Shutdown` row, and the §15.4.2 `READY` → `DRAINING` "(pod terminates)" edge that spec round 6 added all lost to no send on any path (decision 5). The frame means process exit and the kubelet's SIGTERM races it.
- **Hold-timeout close through per-session `Close`.** Ending the runtime through the last member's `Close` in `terminateHeldSession` lost to the pod-scope teardown (CODE-4). Under D3 that `Close` ends nothing, so the runtime would keep a terminated session's credentials with no coordinator.
- **Hold-timeout teardown after pass 1.** It lost to running before pass 1 and after `hold.mu` is released. A `Start` admitted after the clear must see `ended`. A teardown placed right after pass 1 passes every TEST-20 and TEST-3 case; only the `slotCount()` hook discriminates.
- **Transition session.** Letting a `reserved` or pinned idle pod serve one more same-tenant session after an admitted edit takes the pool outside the rule (staged in spec rounds 9 to 11 with a SPEC-8(d) disclosure sentence) lost to failing closed at the next acquisition (decision 6). That session's `sessionIsolationLevel` could report `residualStateWarning: false` in a kept process. A boundary acknowledgment check and a rollout trigger at the edit stay unstaged.
- **SDK `Handler` comment.** The CODE-9 bullet that rewrote `sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go:80-84` lost to the follow-up runtime-SDK proposal (decision 7), because it described a `Run` that the SDK does not implement yet.
- **Skipping the Pod read.** CODE-8's implementor's choice to skip the read on a non-recycling pool is deleted. Under D16 a non-recycling pool is where the read matters most: `AdmitTenantPin` must refuse every pinned pod when `KeepsRuntime` is false.
- **Stamp with the hold-ending `DELETE`.** It lost to stamping at the next acquisition that reads the pod `idle`. The WarmPoolController writes `draining` from any phase, so an early stamp drives `reserved → draining`, an edge §6.2 and `state.ValidTransitions` lack.
- **Stamp inside `AdmitTenantPin`.** The stamp and its `now` parameter lost to a read-only `AdmitTenantPin` and a separate `DrainRefusedPod` (decision 9), because the fallback calls `AdmitTenantPin` under `ClaimIdle`'s row lock.
- **Cross-tenant drain (decision 11).** The D6 tenant-change drain, its per-candidate stamp, its decision-8 cap of one per acquisition, the per-`Claim` cap with an `AllowCrossTenantReuse bool` request field, the `CrossTenantDrain` allowance shared by claim-queue re-entries, `drainOneRefused`, the `kept_runtime_tenant_change` reason, the fallback cross-tenant drain, and the SPEC-8(c) clause that the warm pool provisions an unpinned replacement are all deleted. The drain retired another tenant's pinned pod and gave the requesting session no pod. Rejecting `in-place` with `allowCrossTenantReuse` at pool validation stays unstaged; the field gains a tenant-placement effect only through proposal 0087 part 2.
- **Fallback retry by restoring the row.** CODE-8's former choice to restore the row after a refusal makes `ORDER BY updated_at LIMIT 1` reselect the refused row, so a retry never advances. Projecting the pin into `agent_pod_state.tenant_id` contradicts the §12 schema comment and the mirror sync, and `LIMIT n` locks rows never needed. Refused rows stay locked until the transaction ends (accepted).
- **Pinned-cap alternatives.** A dynamic longest-idle drain, counting pinned idle pods toward the target, and retiring at reserved-hold expiry all lost to the `maxWarm` minus target bound. A separate `lenny_warmpool_pinned_idle_pods` gauge lost to excluding pinned pods from the existing gauge (no new metric).
- **`DrainPinned` list.** Appending pinned drains to `Plan.Drain` lost to a separate list, because every consumer subtracts `len(Drain)` from counts that exclude pinned pods, which drives `status.readyCount` negative.
- **`unassigned` as unpinned.** Reading an absent label or `unassigned` as unpinned lost to the SPEC-8(c) predicate. `AdmitTenantPin` refuses `unassigned` to every tenant, so the planner counts it as pinned; only a missing Pod reads as unpinned.
- **§15.4.1 first-message predicate.** "A `message` whose `sessionId` the runtime has not yet seen is that session's first message" is false: a slot retry lands on a new slot of the same pod, and a resume can reach the same kept process.
- **SPEC-9(g) wording.** The earlier substitute "The runtime process has been given no session" redefined §6.1's "agent process" and made the unedited demotion sentences read as ending the runtime.

Spec-staging rules learned:

- **Sweep every site first.** MISTAKE: redesign1.apply.1 wrote the pod-exit shutdown sequence at two staged sites with different mechanisms and left several statement sites unstaged, which cost round 1 five findings. Classify every site by the blast-radius rule before staging a mechanism.
- **Edit the owning step.** MISTAKE: rounds 1 to 3 put step 1b's UID limit in "What the scrub reaches" and the best-effort parenthetical and never edited step 1b, so the step's "Purge all" contradicted both. State a predicate in the step that owns it.
- **§29 step form.** MISTAKE: round 1 deleted §29.4 step 13's tuple and left the only tupleless step. Every §29 step opens with a participant tuple; a step recording that nothing is sent is `internal`; steps are numbered in the order they occur and never name an earlier point; a message tuple holds only where the message occurs under the step's full condition.
- **Locators.** MISTAKE: redesign1.apply.3 deleted SPEC-4(b), (c), (e), and (f), and (g)'s "In the same paragraph" then resolved to the RBAC paragraph. A locator names its section and label; after a deletion, re-check every surviving "same paragraph" locator.
- **Append-only lettering.** New parts take the next letter (SPEC-9(g) to (k), SPEC-11(d), SPEC-12(h), SPEC-14(g)). Re-lettering breaks every citer.
- **§29.2 prefix.** Steps 24 and 29 of §29.2 also open "On a pod-warm pod" and are per-session; a replace-all of that prefix mis-scopes them.
- **Line-anchored gates.** Never rewrap a §29.2 step; break only the lengthened opening line, because `nonceStatementBlock` finds its anchor on one physical line (step 26a's second line). A tier-11 anchor change is safe only if the anchor is unique on the page.
- **Table cells.** The SPEC-3(a) replacement stays on one physical line inside its cell.
- **No socket name.** Do not name `LENNY_ADAPTER_SOCKET` or any socket or env-var name in staged spec text; N7 requires a MSGSOCK stem and the `CH-MSGSOCK` card names no socket.
- **No restart-policy clause.** Do not re-add "under the pod's `RestartPolicy: Never`" to SPEC-2.
- **No pod-exit signal mechanics.** Do not restate "SIGTERM to agent, wait up to 10s, then SIGKILL" or "the adapter sends SIGTERM on timeout"; the kubelet signals the runtime container.
- **Blast-radius classes.** The P1, P2, P3, OD3, and T rows keep their wording on purpose (the §28.5.3 `shutdown` and `terminate` SIGTERM sentences, the §15.4.3 Graceful-drain cells, exit-code reads, "before spawning" sentences, §5.1 "before runtime starts", spec/26 claude step 6). Classify a new site by the rule before filing it.
- **§4.6.1 pin pointer.** Do not restore rule content (persistence scope, accounting, candidate filter) to the SPEC-4(g) pointer; the copy drifted once.
- **SPEC-11(a).** Do not re-expand it with `sessionId`, acknowledgment, or runtime-exit wording. Its "§15.4" label targets a sentence under §15.4.3, which is unique, so it is not a false citation.
- **Hold-timeout close homes.** SPEC-2 names the close in one clause and SPEC-18(b) states it. Keep the reason (credentials without a coordinator) in SPEC-18(b) only.
- **SPEC-18(b) credential clause.** It relies on the runtime ending its process at `CH-MSGSOCK` EOF, which only §15.7's `Run` states. The shipped per-session close had the same effect, so it is not a regression; do not ask for the `terminate` frame (decisions 5 and 10). This closes spec-recheck.3.review-security.1's OPEN.
- **"Configurable only".** That wording in SPEC-8(f) or DOC-4 reads as an admission gate. Do not add `recycle.enabled` to CODE-9's `runtimestore.go` comment.
- **endReservedHolds scope.** It ends holds of any tenant, while SPEC-4(h), SPEC-9(j), and SPEC-12(g) sit in same-tenant rebind context; SPEC-8(d) covers any tenant. Narrowing SPEC-8(d) to the rebind opens a drift.
- **Label forward references.** The SPEC-1 to SPEC-4 steps cite §5.2 labels that the SPEC-7 and SPEC-8 steps create; no gate fails between them, and every spec-block order leaves one.
- **Wrapped anchors.** §29.4 steps 6 and 12, the §28.5.3 `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` **Degradation.** sentence (which SPEC-14(c) and (d) both quote), the §15.7 `TaskID` comment, and `catalog.go`'s "claim-hold" / "TTL expires" wrap across lines; match across the wrap before filing a missing anchor.
- **Glob.** `$P/*spec-changes.md` matches both spec-changes.md and non-spec-changes.md; name the file.
- **Historical step numbers.** Dated entries that name old step numbers stay as written; a sweep that "fixes" them rewrites history.
- **Refuted lists.** `scratchpad/cp-log/0079-redesign/history/spec.r{2,3,4}.history.md` hold the refuted findings; check them before filing on decision 1 or §29.2 step 32. The §5.2 "(pod return to pool)" refutation rested on a wrong citation, and SPEC-8(c) now stages the deletion.

Not defects (do not file):

- **Non-exclusive sentences.** spec/04:418 ("the unhealthy-threshold drain derives from ... drain-request"), the §6.2 `idle ──→ draining` edge (a §9.1 non-goal), spec/04:407 (claim-deletion list), the §4.7 `StartSession` row and §7.1 step 15 (loose before this proposal on concurrent pods), spec/05:493 step 7, the spec/06:122 `reserved ──→ claimed` label, the **Partial occupancy.** rebind sentence, the §5.2 concurrent-acknowledgment closing sentence, the "runtime close fails → leaked" rows, the §29.2 step 5 scan, the `Drained` `PoolWarmingUp` reason, and the §5.2 pod-retirement list's omission of the `vm-restart` retire (which lives at step 7).
- **Accepted outcomes.** The pinned tenant's `PoolWarmingUp` 503 while only pinned pods are idle; a hung runtime on a concurrent recycling pool reported live until the whole-pod trigger; an outside-rule pinned idle pod that sorts after the first admissible pod is never stamped (a sweep re-opens D14); a pod whose bind failed after the pin stamp on a `KeepsRuntime: false` pool is drained at the next acquisition; the PDB counting pinned idle pods; a concluded variant pool deleted at once when only pinned pods are idle (cascade GC).
- **Pre-existing races.** The WarmPoolController pinned drain against a claim CREATE (the excess drain has the same race); two WarmPoolController phase writers flapping on a drain-requested bound pod; a stale-resolution claim racing a D16 stamp (the drained pod keeps its session).
- **Failed-start drain is a claim `DELETE`.** `Binder.failPhase` only deletes the claim, so the pod drains only if the WarmPoolController projected `claimed` first; under WarmPoolController lag a `reserved` rebind's dead-runtime pod returns to `idle` pinned and self-heals at that tenant's next acquisition. This corrects "reclaimOnFailure drains the pod". On the two-step API the projection lands first except across a controller outage.
- **Pre-existing spec conflicts.** §4.6.1 `maxWarm > 0` against §10.7 setting `maxWarm` 0; §10.1.4 "Before exiting" and `AdapterTerminating`; §10.7 `active → paused` "maxWarm unchanged" against the planner; the §7.1 `scrubPolicy` row against `scrubPolicyForPool`; §4.7.11 `CrashLoopBackOff`.
- **Kept runtime at a session end.** Under decisions 3 and 5 a session end, a budget end, and a §11.4 full revoke send the kept runtime no stop signal; the SDK follow-up owns the signal, and a finding that asks for one re-opens those decisions. A revoked user's kept process is the same-tenant residual D5 accepts (see the Open UNVERIFIED on the revoke disposition).
- **§15.4.6 Full deadline exit and §15.7 `OnTerminate`.** A Full runtime exiting on `deadline_signal` is a legal exit that retires the pod (OD3); `Handler.OnTerminate` and `TerminationReason` belong to the SDK follow-up.
- **Later-session connections.** A later session keeps the first session's MCP connections and `lifecycle_support`; D9 and the §9.1 manifest bullet cover it, and concurrent pods behave the same way.
- **Concurrent `vm-restart`.** SPEC-8(d)'s "Otherwise, the recycling configurations outside the rule keep no runtime process" is loose for a concurrent `vm-restart` pool, which already needs the field; not filed.
- **Code-only notes.** `verifyIntegrationLevel` caches per replica and may read a Standard runtime as Basic on a later session; `MCPRuntime` has no production wiring; direct-mode token loss is decision 1 and is not a quota bypass; the best-effort paragraph's resolver-process claim is unfiled because where such a process runs is unstated.

Docs and tests:

- **Resumed session.** Do not write "first session" for a resumed session in any docs deliverable; a resume takes the tenant-keyed claim path and can reach a kept process. The concepts.md resume clause reads "restores the session from the checkpoint on the new pod into the runtime process the pod started with".
- **Non-sites.** `docs/runtime-author-guide/lifecycle.md:229` is not a DOC-6 site; `concepts.md:398` mirrors the P3 §5.1 sentence and keeps its wording; `docs/reference/glossary.md:275` names the §6.1 SDK process and is true.
- **internal.md step 6.** A session on a non-recycled pod still drains to `TERMINATED` through `DRAINING`, and the DOC-6 implementor must also restate step 6's "The adapter transitions to `TERMINATED`." `docs/api/internal.md:157` (`clean_exit`) is unclassified in the blast-radius table.
- **Locate docs by quoted text.** No gate covers "no runtime cooperation" in docs, so a missed DOC-2 or DOC-3 edit passes; never locate a target by position in a line whose other sentences another instruction deletes. DOC-6 replacement text must avoid every TEST-16 phrase.
- **DOC-4 kept phrases.** The security-principles `in-place` rewrite keeps "`recycle.acknowledgeMicrovmResidualState: true`"; multi-tenancy.md:61's `vm-restart` sentence keeps "retired at the recycle boundary", "fresh replacement pod", and "warm pool".
- **DOC-2 predicate.** DOC-2's reader-vocabulary restatement of the SPEC-8(d) predicate is accepted (non-spec.2.review-single-source.1). Cite SPEC-8(d), never SPEC-8(b), for the predicate or the refusal.
- **Tier-9 webhook.** A Kind tier-9 case cannot use the webhook's absence as its discriminator; assert at the claim layer (claim placement, bound pod).
- **Kind warm counts.** A test that needs `maxWarm` above `minWarm` uses the admin API (`warmCount` plus `bootstrapMinWarm`). Do not `DELETE .../bootstrap-override` before TEST-21's `maxSessionsPerPod: 1` update, or `minWarm` returns to `warmCount` and the scan binds an unpinned pod.
- **`WarmPoolOversized`.** Do not add `lenny_pool_max_warm`, a recycling-pool gauge, or a label value; do not delete the rule, because `TestCatalogIncludesFirstWeekTuningRules_spec_17_8_2` requires it.
- **CODE-8 pointers.** Do not re-expand CODE-8's pointers to §4.4 with a description of the target.

### [non-spec.4.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: CODE-10 lets the pool reconciler read Pod labels and SandboxClaim existence even though it watches neither Pods nor claims. That stays level-correct. The OccupancyReconciler watches SandboxClaims through the same manager cache (pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:308) and writes the Sandbox phase only after it has seen the claim change. So when that Sandbox event triggers a pool reconcile, the cached claim already reflects the delete. The only ordering gap is pin-before-claim during an acquisition in flight, and CODE-10's uncached APIReader confirmation covers it. FACT: CODE-10's pinned drain goes through drainSandbox, which uses patchSandboxDraining with requireIdle=false, the same call the excess drain uses. Its race with a pinned tenant's claim CREATE is the accepted pre-existing race, not a new one. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:623-643

### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in non-spec round 4 — BECAUSE the only r3-to-r4 change is a DOC-6 docs hunk (concepts.md:79 and :97 resume wording), and the remaining performance leads are either refuted already (CODE-10 uncached claim Get per reconcile; CODE-8 per-candidate Pod GETs, including the in-transaction fallback reads) or adjudicated (decision 8, uncapped D16 drain; decision 9, pin read inside the row lock; D14, zero pinned-idle bound and the pod churn it implies on admin-API pools) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the D16 re-stamp churn (rejected: bounded transient, the WarmPoolController pod reconciler consumes the stamp on the next Pod event, and decision 8 settles the uncapped drain), filing the CODE-10 cached Pod List deep-copy cost per reconcile (rejected: efficiency only; no spec budget it breaks).
FACT: Every production construction of a claim request is covered by CODE-8's KeepsRuntime threading: `exclusiveBindRequest` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_request.go:146), the `ResumeRequest` literal (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:97, resolved through `ResolvePool` with `s.poolPolicyReader()` at :73), and `Binder.connect`'s `ClaimRequest` (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/claimconnect.go:57) from bindclaim.go:56 and resume.go:121. `pkg/podregistry/crd.go:235` calls `CreateClaim` directly and never reaches `Claimer.Claim`, so no production path sends a zero-valued KeepsRuntime that would mass-drain pinned pods. EVIDENCE: grep of pkg/ and cmd/ for `ClaimRequest{`, `BindRequest{`, `ResumeRequest{`, `.connect(`
FACT: The D16 drain stamp is consumed promptly. The pod reconciler watches all managed Pods with no annotation-filtering predicate (pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:757-762), and `reconcileDrainRequest` patches the Sandbox to `draining` from any phase (:609-635). So the re-stamp window lasts one Pod-event reconcile rather than one 60 s pool requeue. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:294-300, :609-635
FACT: KeepsRuntime adds no new store dependency. `poolPolicyMirror.PoolPolicy` already does one `pools.Get` per resolution (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:55-62), and an error fails resolution rather than resolving false, so a Postgres failover does not make it resolve false. No part of the design reads Redis. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:55-62
USEFUL [Tree facts: Drain consumer]: confirmed `requireIdle=false` on the drain-request path, which saved re-deriving the D16 stamp lifetime.

### [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]
- DECISION: no reliability findings in non-spec round 4 — BECAUSE the r3-to-r4 diff is the DOC-6 concepts.md resume clause only, and a re-walk of the hold-timeout teardown (CODE-4 between the `hold.mu` release and pass 1, holdstate.go:177-190), the D16 acquisition refusal (`endReservedHolds`, `AdmitTenantPin`, `DrainRefusedPod`, the fallback stamps after `ClaimIdle`), and CODE-10's read-failure handling found every path terminating with an idempotent or precondition-guarded write — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing SPEC-7(a)'s "the gateway drains the pod" as false on the resume path (see WATCHOUT); rejected because the WPC orphan GC drains the pod within the orphan timeout and the leak predates this proposal. (2) filing CODE-10's fail-the-reconcile-on-read-error as stalling the Postgres fallback by starving the mirror; rejected because `syncMirror` runs before `plan.Compute` (controller.go:247 vs :257), so the pin reads land after it.
- WATCHOUT: a Resume that rebinds a tenant's `reserved` pod whose runtime died during the hold fails at the adapter's `Runtime.Start` (pkg/adapter/resume.go:161), and on a `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` pool the gateway neither deletes the claim nor drains the pod: `failResume` only compensates (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:264-273) and `accountResumeSlotFailure` returns at an empty SlotID (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:308-311). The pod stays `claimed` until the WPC orphan GC drains it (default 5 min, pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:34). SPEC-7(a) and D4 cite only the StartSession path (bindlaunch.go, reclaim.go). Pre-existing for any resume failure; do not refile without showing an unbounded outcome. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:157-159
- FACT: `DeleteOnHoldExpiry` returns (false, nil) on NotFound and (true, nil) on Conflict (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/bindingstate.go:282-295), so two replicas' `endReservedHolds` on the same reserved pod, or one racing the holder's TTL timer, never fail an acquisition. — EVIDENCE: bindingstate.go:285-292
- FACT: every production `BindRequest` and `ResumeRequest` is built from a `ResolvePool` match through `s.poolPolicyReader()` (pod_claim.go:111, pod_launch.go:124 and :252, finalize.go:227, resume_rebind.go:75), and `poolPolicyMirror.PoolPolicy` reads the pool store directly with no cache, so no path reaches `Claimer.Claim` with a zero-valued `KeepsRuntime` on an acknowledged pool and mass-ends holds or stamps drains. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:55-62; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_request.go:144
- FACT: service-mode sessions are claimless (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pod_claim.go:140-142), so the stateless router's tenant labels on service pods never meet `AdmitTenantPin` or `DrainRefusedPod`. — EVIDENCE: pod_claim.go:136-142
- USEFUL [spec.18.review-reliability.1 WATCHOUT]: the claim-DELETE-as-drain trap framed the resume-path WATCHOUT above without re-deriving the projection rules.
- USEFUL [spec.16.review-reliability.1 FACT on StampDrainRequest NotFound]: settled that a D16 stamp on a vanished pod cannot fail an acquisition.

### [non-spec.4.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding in non-spec round 4 — BECAUSE the only r3-to-r4 change is the DOC-6 resume clause (docs prose, no control), and re-walking the directed areas found every bound with a trusted, durable source: the pin is a gateway-written Pod label whose removal and {A}→{B} change the fail-closed webhook refuses (pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:197-205); `unassigned` has no writer and AdmitTenantPin refuses it; KeepsRuntime comes from the pool store, and a missing row resolves false, which refuses reuse (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:55-62); runtime_live can only retire a pod or keep same-tenant reuse — ALTERNATIVES: a TOCTOU in which another tenant pins the pod between AdmitTenantPin and CreateClaim was rejected, because that tenant's whole session would have to run and its claim be deleted in the window, and the later pin stamp is then refused by the webhook (Forbidden, which stampPodTenant does not tolerate; it tolerates only NotFound and Conflict, slotclaimer.go:70-73); the USER_REVOKED and full-revoke kept-process cases were not filed (Standing context, "Kept runtime at a session end").
FACT: every production idle-acquisition site is one that CODE-8 names. `Binder.connect` is the only `podclaim.Claimer` construction (claimconnect.go:45), reached from bindclaim.go:56 and resume.go:121. `CRDPodRegistry.ClaimPod` and `PostgresPodRegistry.ClaimPod` have no production caller, and `AgentSandboxPodLifecycleManager` is wired only as the orphan-session phase reader (cmd/lenny-gateway/reconciler_workers.go:92). — EVIDENCE: grep of `.ClaimPod(` and `podclaim.Claimer{` under pkg/ and cmd/
FACT: `onHoldTimeout` is the only coordinator-loss termination path. `deregisterStartedSessions` and `terminateHeldSession` have no other caller, so CODE-4's teardown placement (after hold.mu is released at :187, before pass 1 at :190) covers every firing that finds the hold active. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:177-211
CORRECTS [Standing context, Tree facts, "Gateway reads"]: the chart grants the gateway SA get/list/watch/patch on Pods (charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:177-178). The spec §4.6.3 **Gateway ServiceAccount RBAC grants:** paragraph grants only get/patch (spec/04_system-components.md:632). So "no list/watch" is true of the spec and false of the chart, which is pre-existing drift. A Pod LIST remedy still needs the §4.6.3 edit to be spec-conformant.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-security.1]: its FACTs on tenant IDs that are never empty and on the completeness of the BindRequest and ResumeRequest literals saved re-deriving both.

### [non-spec.4.review-test-coverage.1]

- DECISION: filed one finding, TEST-14 cannot reach an `idle` pod pinned to `acme`, so it does not discriminate CODE-8 — BECAUSE under SPEC-8(c) the pinned-idle bound on the only Kind sequential recycling pool (`task-mode-echo-pool`, warmCount 2, so minWarm = maxWarm = 2) is zero, and the WarmPoolController drains a pinned pod as soon as it projects `idle`; inside the hold the pod is `reserved` and the idle scan's phase filter skips it; and the scan returns at the first admissible pod in name order — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the webhook premise (already fixed, a different cause); filing the CODE-4 `runtime_teardown_failed` Warn branch as an untested error path (skipped: the teardown sets `ended` and closes the connection before the listener close that can error, so the security property holds whatever the branch does; nice-to-have); filing the untested `SubprocessExecutor.ServesNextSession` (dev-loop only, one-line return; nice-to-have).
- FACT: TEST-11's timing holds against the zero pinned-idle bound: the case waits for the claim to read `reserved` and creates B at once (tests/tier5_e2e_kind/execution_modes_test.go:168-171), well inside the 10 s hold. Its new "restart count is still 0" assertion cannot fail under `RestartPolicy: Never`, but the landing assertion already discriminates, so it is redundant rather than a gap. EVIDENCE: tests/tier5_e2e_kind/execution_modes_test.go:168-180
- WATCHOUT: any Kind case that needs an `idle` pod pinned to a tenant must lower `minWarm` below `maxWarm` through the admin API (TEST-21 second subtest is the model) and make that pod the pool's only idle Sandbox; otherwise the pinned pod drains on `task-mode-echo-pool` before any acquisition reads it, and the case passes without CODE-8. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/poolscaling/poolstoresource.go:107-108; spec-changes.md:326-330
- USEFUL [standing context "Kind warm counts"]: the trap entry pointed straight at the TEST-14 gap.

### [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]
FACT: The WarmPoolController's Reconcile runs syncMirror (controller.go:247) before plan.Compute (:257), so a CODE-10 pin-read error that fails the reconcile before the plan still leaves the agent_pod_state mirror synced; the fallback's "mirror Sync rewrites a committed row" recovery (non-spec §4.4) does not depend on the pin reads succeeding. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:247-257
FACT: Under the default bound of zero (D14), every hold expiry without a same-tenant return costs one pinned drain plus one replacement create. That is the same pod churn as a non-recycling pool plus the recycle claim PATCHes, and at the Tier 3 rate of about 30 claims/s it stays under the Tier 3 pod-creation limiter of 80/200. spec/04:475's "amortizes CREATE and DELETE traffic across every session of the occupancy episode" stays literally true. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:475; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1151
FACT: After a hold-timeout teardown (CODE-4), the pod is no less available than before. runtimekit does not redial (pkg/runtimekit/transport.go has no reconnect path) and agent pods are RestartPolicy Never, so the shipped design also leaves a dead runtime after the hold timeout. The only change is that Start now fails fast instead of waiting AcceptTimeout. — EVIDENCE: pkg/runtimekit/transport.go:44-52
FACT: KeepsRuntime cannot mass-drain pinned pods on a Postgres failover. poolPolicyMirror.PoolPolicy returns an error on any non-NotFound read, and foldPoolPolicy fails resolution on it. Only ErrNotFound resolves false, and InspectForRecycle yields no recycle for a missing row, so no pinned pod arises there. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:55-62; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:358-366
USEFUL [Standing context, Gateway reads / Controller reads facts]: these saved re-deriving the QPS budgets; every remaining read-cost concern is already refuted as efficiency (history r7).

A new claim with the same deterministic name fails the old timer's UID precondition (Conflict, aborted). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/recycle/holdcoordinator.go:139; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/bindingstate.go:270-296
FACT: CODE-10's pin reads run after `syncMirror` in `Reconcile`. A failed pin read therefore stalls replenishment and status for that pass, but it does not stall the agent_pod_state mirror the fallback depends on. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:241-248

The spec-changes §5 row's "the next report carries `runtime_live: false`" is loose, but the pod is still never reused, so this was not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:308-312, :499
WATCHOUT: The Start-accept versus hold-timeout teardown race (the teardown takes a nil `conn` while an accept is in flight) cannot happen. ### [non-spec.7.review-security.1]

Security sweep of non-spec round 7 (diff since r5-prefix: only the §10 tier-8 sentence). No finding met the bar.

- FACT: The `sessions_served` counter is gateway-side, and its only trigger is the adapter's report. `IncrementSessionsServed` runs only in `ScrubReporter.RecordSessionScrub`, and the `ReportSessionScrub` RPC handler is its only caller. The two UNVERIFIED readings are therefore both half right. This proposal does not change the source, and the bound is same-tenant, so it is not a security finding. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/scrubreport_server.go:93, :455-463
- FACT: The §11.4 full-revoke fan-out sends `Shutdown` with reason `USER_REVOKED` and no `Recycle`, so that request never starts a scrub. `answerShutdown` starts the whole-pod scrub on any `Shutdown` that carries `Recycle`, even one whose outcome is ABSENT. As a result, a later `Binder.Release` with a non-`failed` disposition (such as `cancelled`) on a recycling pool still scrubs the pod and can reserve it. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:127; pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go:939-950; pkg/adapter/session.go:498-500; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go:69-91
- UNVERIFIED: whether a `Release` actually follows the revoke for a revoked session. The admin handler only cancels the session in SessionStore. This narrows the open revoke-disposition entry but does not close it. Under "Not defects", the outcome is the same-tenant residual in either case.
- FACT: `pkg/podregistry` `CRDPodRegistry.ClaimPod` is a second idle-claim path with no pin read (crd.go:235). Nothing under cmd/ wires it, so it is not an acquisition site CODE-8 must cover. EVIDENCE: grep of cmd/ for podregistry returns nothing.
- FACT: Every production `ClaimRequest` comes from `Binder.connect` (claimconnect.go:57) or `SlotClaimer.reserveSlot`. Every `BindRequest` comes from `exclusiveBindRequest` (pod_claim.go:205, pod_launch.go:171/:303, finalize.go:279), and the only `ResumeRequest` is resume_rebind.go:97. No acquisition path can reach a kept pod without the `KeepsRuntime` threading CODE-8 stages.
- USEFUL [Standing context "Kept runtime at a session end"]: it saved re-filing the revoke and budget-end stop-signal question.

- FACT: `resolveWarm` lowers `minWarm` only via `ForceZeroMinWarm` (paused experiment), an active `scaleToZero` window, the formula once demand is observed, or a `bootstrapMinWarm` override; `maxWarm` is written from `cfg.MaxWarm` (= `warmCount`) — D14's rewording matches — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/poolscaling/controller.go `resolveWarm`, `pool.Spec.MaxWarm = cfg.MaxWarm`.

It is bounded by the slot-health trigger or by orphan-claim predicate 1 (`bound`, no active session, `claimOrphanTimeout` 5 min) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:488-503; spec/04_system-components.md:517.

SPEC-2 words the close as happening when the adapter "terminates every session it started". No harm traced: a pod whose sessions all released during the hold has no reachable coordinator, so it retires through orphan GC or the missing report. The non-spec loop should confirm this is intended — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:177-194; non-spec-changes.md CODE-4.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.review-performance.1]

FACT: The non-spec-r8 and spec-recheck-r4-prefix snapshots differ from the live proposal only in the D14 sentence (the added experiment-variant `initialMinWarm` input); non-spec-changes.md and summary.md are byte-identical to non-spec-r8. EVIDENCE: `diff -ru -x '*.review-log*.md'` against both snapshots.
FACT: CODE-10 adds no informer. The pool manager already caches Pods (`pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:758`) and SandboxClaims (`pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:308`), and the controller's reconcile context has no deadline, so the "other" 10 QPS limiter only delays the APIReader Gets and never fails them (`pkg/controller/ratelimit/ratelimit.go:35-36`, `:157-165`; no WithTimeout in `pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go`). A finding that CODE-10 can fail a reconcile through the rate limiter has no basis.
FACT: `KeepsRuntime` resolution reads the pool store through `poolPolicyMirror.PoolPolicy` on every `ResolvePool` call (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:56-63`), the same Postgres read that resolution already does. A read error fails resolution (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:362-365`). A Postgres failover therefore cannot turn into `KeepsRuntime: false`, a mass `endReservedHolds`, or a mass D16 stamp. A missing row also resolves false; that path is fail-closed and already exists today.
FACT: The gateway's ClaimSlot idle pass reads the Pod before `reserveSlot` (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:476-505`), so CODE-8's added Pod Get holds no Redis slot counter and no Postgres advisory lock. A Redis reset adds no failure mode, because the proposal stores no value in Redis.
FACT: No Postgres `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` is configured anywhere in spec/, pkg/, or charts/. The fallback's Pod Get inside `ClaimIdle`'s transaction therefore cannot be killed by a transaction-idle timeout. Its cost is an efficiency matter only, and the history already refutes it.
DECISION: Returned no findings. BECAUSE: every performance item in this lens (the CODE-10 uncached claim Get, the CODE-8 pin Gets inside and outside the transaction, and D16 re-stamping) has been refuted as efficiency (history r1 lines 59-60), or is an accepted outcome in Standing context. Failover tracing (Postgres, Redis, coordinator hold, WPC leader) found nothing less reliable than the shipped design. ALTERNATIVES: (1) Etcd write amplification from the bound-zero pinned drain on recycling pools (an unreturned session pays both the recycle claim PATCHes and non-recycling pod churn, about 2 extra Sandbox status writes per session against the Tier 3 120 QPS status limiter). Not filed: the spec/04 §4.6.1 write-estimate paragraph (`spec/04_system-components.md:475`) stays literally true, and the cost follows from decision D14 (option D), whose follow-up owns the bound. (2) `endReservedHolds` serially doing 2xR apiserver calls on the first acquisition after a pool edit. Not filed: it happens once per operator edit and is efficiency only.
WATCHOUT: A future performance lens will again be tempted to file the bound-zero churn on admin-API recycling pools as an etcd budget finding. Its only remedies are a pinned-idle bound or TTL, or a spec/04 estimate edit. The caller directive forbids the first two and the decision of 2026-09-30 owns the behaviour. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md D14; `spec/04_system-components.md:475`.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.review-security.1]

DECISION: no security finding this round — BECAUSE the delta since non-spec-r8 is only the D14 `initialMinWarm` clause (no non-spec or summary change), and a full sweep of the acquisition refusal (D16), tenant pin admission, hold-timeout teardown, admission rule, and RBAC found no bypass or regressed control — ALTERNATIVES: filing the unstaged revoke comment (below) was rejected because it is a Go comment outside the (d) surfaces and a P1-class claim.
FACT: settles the UNVERIFIED `sessions_served` source. The adapter's `ReportSessionScrub` is the only writer; the gateway increments on receipt. In the sidecar model the runtime container cannot suppress that report, and embedded pods are never recycled (no `ScrubOps`). On a `maxSessionsPerPod: 1` pool a lost report leaves the count at 0, so `Decide` can reserve the pod. D16 still refuses it: `KeepsRuntime` is false there, so the next acquisition ends the hold and drains the pod. FACT: the gateway's pool resolution reads the pool store directly on every resolution, and a read error propagates. `KeepsRuntime` therefore cannot be stale-cached, and an outage cannot flip it to false. FACT: the tenant-label webhook refuses `{tenant} → unset`. Only the gateway SA can set the label on an unset pod, and no code writes `unassigned`. ### [spec.21.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: `Binder.Release` takes the recycle path for every non-`failed` terminal on a recycling pool, so a sidecar pod whose first session is cancelled or expires before `StartSession` is admitted reaches the recycle boundary with `connected` false; `ServesNextSession` then reports false and the pod retires with `runtime_not_live`. This settles the "pending dial" half of the standing UNVERIFIED entry: the outcome is fail-safe (one wasted pod), not a reuse of an unverified runtime, and below the bar — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go:77 (`if result.Recycle && disposition != dispositionFailed`); non-spec-changes.md:146 (`ServesNextSession` "false before accept").
FACT: The decision-12 edits (SPEC-8(d) stamp-failure sentences, D16, §3 acquisition paragraph, edge rows 97 and 100) agree: only the idle scan stamps, the fallback stamps nothing, and a failed claim read or stamp is logged and the scan continues. Any acquisition that refuses a pod in the fallback has already refused it in its primary scan (the fallback runs only after `ErrNoIdlePod`), so "the next acquisition that refuses the pod stamps it" does not contradict "the fallback stamps nothing" — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/claimconnect.go:63-67.
FACT: Every "above"/"below" cross-reference that SPEC-5, SPEC-6, SPEC-7, and SPEC-8 stage inside §5.2 resolves in the right direction against the current line order (tenant pinning :442, scrub procedure :475, retirement policy :502, concurrent acknowledgment :533, slot retry policy :569, mode_factor :593) — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md at those lines.
USEFUL [Standing context, Not defects]: the PoolWarmingUp 503, the `Drained` reason, the failed-start claim DELETE, and the stale-resolution stamp race entries saved re-deriving four end-to-end traces this round.

DECISION: no spec-scoped findings under the performance and failure-mode lens in spec round 21 — BECAUSE the only spec-changes delta since the converged run is decision 12 (D16 and SPEC-8(d): a failed claim read or stamp is logged and the scan continues; the Postgres fallback stamps nothing), which removes a write path rather than adding one, and its new citations hold (`claimconnect.go:64-67` runs the fallback only after `ErrNoIdlePod`; `claimer.go:104-109` is the pool-wide Sandbox LIST; `fallbackclaim.go:58-75` is the mirror-lag precondition) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the claim-queue re-entry storm on a pool whose only idle pods are pinned (each 250 ms re-entry repeats P Pod GETs in the scan plus P inside the fallback transaction, and on an outside-rule pool P claim GETs and P re-stamp PATCHes; rejected: its remedies are code (refusal memo, skip an already-stamped pod) owned by the OPEN [non-spec-changes.md] CODE-8 pin reads and D16 re-stamp entries, and the in-transaction read is decision 9); filing the SPEC-8(d) "stamps its annotation" sentence as mandating the re-stamp (rejected: a pod already carrying the annotation satisfies it, so a non-spec skip does not contradict the spec).
FACT: failure-mode trace for this staging. Postgres failover: `KeepsRuntime` resolution fails rather than reading false, so no mass drain; the fallback is skipped on mirror lag. Redis reset: nothing the staging adds reads Redis (pin is a Pod label, counters are Postgres `agent_pod_state`). Coordinator handoff past the hold timeout: the CODE-4 pod-scope teardown ends the sidecar runtime exactly where the shipped per-session `Close` did, and the pod now retires through `runtime_live: false` instead of being reused into a failed start, so it is strictly no worse. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md D10, D16; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/fallbackclaim.go:58-75.

### [spec.21.review-reliability.1]

DECISION: no reliability findings in spec round 21 — BECAUSE the only spec-staging delta since the converged snapshot is decision 12 (D16, the §3 acquisition paragraph, two §5 rows, the SPEC-8(d) stamp sentences, and §12). Every recovery path it touches is bounded. A failed claim read writes no stamp, because the stamp needs a NotFound. A failed stamp is retried by a later idle scan that reads the pod `idle`. A pinned idle pod that no later acquisition refuses is still drained by the SPEC-8(c) bound or by the certificate replacement — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing "the next acquisition that refuses the pod stamps it" as contradicting "the fallback stamps nothing". Rejected: the fallback runs only after the same acquisition's idle scan (claimconnect.go:64-67), so a refusal that reads the Sandbox `idle` always passes through the scan. (2) filing the claim-queue re-entry (every 250 ms) re-issuing a failed stamp against a struggling apiserver as a stampede. Rejected: it is performance-lens territory, an OPEN entry already tracks it ("D16 re-stamp"), and it is the consequence decision 12 adjudicated.
FACT: a fail-fast `Start` error on a dead runtime cannot be mistaken for a slot-bind refusal, so `reclaimOnFailure` still runs `failPhase`. `IsSlotBindRefusal` matches only the two sentinels that `translateSlotBindRefusal` recovers from a gRPC status detail, and it does not match on the status code alone — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go:50-53, :71-75; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:28-35
FACT: on the create-time `ClaimSlot` and `BindReservedSlot` paths, a concurrent slot whose `Start` fails on a dead runtime has no retry budget. A transient reason keeps the retryable SESSION_CREATION_FAILED fallback with Retry-After, so a dead runtime during the hold costs that client a retry, the session is not lost, and the slot-health window still accrues toward the whole-pod trigger — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:102-142, :176-190
WATCHOUT: for the hold-timeout case, SPEC-2's "the adapter reports it at the next recycle boundary and refuses the next session's start" holds only conditionally. The terminated sessions end in failure, so §5.2's "A session that ends in failure or a crash also retires the pod" (or the orphan-claim GC) retires the pod, usually with no recycle boundary. The pod is still never reused. Do not file it — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:508; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:58
USEFUL [non-spec.7.review-reliability.1 WATCHOUT on the hold-timeout Shutdown answering without a teardown]: it settled the SPEC-2 hold-timeout wording without re-tracing session.go.
USEFUL [Standing context, Not defects, "Failed-start drain is a claim DELETE"]: it closed the reserved-rebind WarmPoolController-lag case, which the fail-fast `Start` makes more likely; the bound drain covers it on admin-API pools.

### [spec.21.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding in spec round 21 — BECAUSE the redesign delta (decision 12, log and continue; the Postgres fallback no longer stamps) only removes drain stamps on a pod every acquisition already refuses, so no isolation control weakens: AdmitTenantPin still refuses every pinned pod when KeepsRuntime is false, a non-NotFound Pod read fails the claim closed (non-spec §4.4), and DrainRefusedPod writes nothing on a claim-read error — ALTERNATIVES: filing SPEC-8(d)'s "the next acquisition that refuses the pod stamps it" as contradicting the fallback's no-stamp clause (rejected: the fallback runs only after the same acquisition's idle scan returned ErrNoIdlePod, so that scan already refused and tried to stamp the pod; claimconnect.go:64-67); filing the missing ConcurrentWorkspaceCredentialSharing-style pool condition for kept-runtime recycling pools, where a lingering session-1 subprocess in the sidecar runtime container can read the next same-tenant session's 0440 credential file (rejected: SPEC-15(a)/(c) list the case under the acknowledgment, which is the gate the concurrent case also relies on; less strict than possible is not a finding).
FACT: KeepsRuntime's pool-side source is a direct poolstore Get per resolution, not a cache or Redis; NotFound resolves found=false and other errors propagate, so there is no store-outage relaxation path. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:55-62
FACT: the tenant-label webhook code already permits {tenant} → unassigned for the WarmPoolController SA on any pool (no allowCrossTenantReuse gate), so SPEC-4(d)'s ungated wording matches the tree; `unassigned → {tenant}` is refused. — EVIDENCE: pkg/admission/label_immutability/label_immutability.go:186-207
USEFUL [non-spec-recheck.1.review-security.1]: its settled `sessions_served` source (adapter ReportSessionScrub, gateway increments on receipt) closed the maxSessionsPerPod trust-source question without re-deriving it.

### [spec.23.review-reliability.1]
FACT: A transient pool-store read error does not resolve `KeepsRuntime` false. `poolPolicyMirror.PoolPolicy` returns NotFound as found=false, but any other `Get` error propagates, and `foldPoolPolicy` fails `ResolvePool` with it. An apiserver or Postgres blip therefore cannot trigger the D16 hold-end or drain on a pool that keeps its runtime. Only a missing or soft-deleted pool row, or an unwired reader, leaves the field false. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:55-62, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:357-367
FACT: A drain-request stamp that lands on a pod an acquisition with a stale configuration has just claimed takes the legal `claimed → draining` edge. `reconcileDrainRequest` calls `patchSandboxDraining` with requireIdle=false. The bound session then completes and the pod retires, which matches SPEC-8(d)'s "session already bound when the update lands" clause, so the stamp has no reserved-to-draining hazard beyond the one D16 already guards. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:609-622
FACT: The claim-queue retry of a failing D16 stamp is event-driven, because `runWithQueue` re-enters as pods free, so it is not a tight loop against the apiserver. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pod_claim.go:205-212
FACT: The failed-start drain cited by D4 and SPEC-7(a) is skipped only for the two typed slot-bind refusals (`IsSlotBindRefusal`). An `ended` error from `Runtime.Start` is not one of them, so the pod drains. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go:50-53, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:28-35
DECISION: Round 23 reliability returned no findings. BECAUSE: this round's diff is a wording fix ("stamps no drain request"), and tracing the hold-end DELETE, the stamp retry, the fallback pin, the hold-timeout teardown, and the runtime_live sample through restart and race found no recovery path that loses work, leaks a resource, or stalls. ALTERNATIVES: filing the apiserver load from the uncapped drain stamps; rejected, because decisions 8 and 12 settle it and acquisitions pace the calls.

### [f1.human-decisions.14]
- DECISION: summary open decision 14 (whether the Postgres fallback claim stamps the pods it refuses for drain) resolved by the open-decisions review, unanimous, to Option A: the fallback refuses through `AdmitTenantPin` and writes no drain stamp, and the next acquisition that reads the pod `idle` from its Sandbox stamps it. The staging already carries A at D16 (spec-changes.md:37), SPEC-8(d) (spec-changes.md:373-375), CODE-8 (non-spec-changes.md:80), and TEST-13 (non-spec-changes.md:327), so no staged file changed. Authority: the fallback runs only after `Claimer.Claim` returns `ErrNoIdlePod` (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/claimconnect.go:63-67), which it does only after scanning every pool Sandbox (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:124-162); the drain-request reconciler writes `draining` from any phase (pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:620); and neither §6.2 (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:122-125) nor `ValidTransitions` (pkg/sandbox/state/state.go:184-185) has a `reserved → draining` edge, so a stamp from a lagging mirror row can drive a forbidden edge. A's residual is the deferred stamp decision 12 already accepted.
- FACT: summary.md `## Open decisions for human to make` now reads "The human adjudicated decisions 1 to 12 and A to C, and spec-changes.md §12 records them. No decision is open." Entry 14 was removed. spec-changes.md §12 "No decision is open" (spec-changes.md:564) is now true and was not edited. No checklist change.
- CORRECTS [reconcile.checklist.5, WATCHOUT]: closed. §12 and summary entry 14 no longer disagree. The Settled bullet **Failed D16 stamp (decision 12)** already states that the fallback writes no drain stamp and needs no change.

### [non-spec.8.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: TEST-21's tier-2 podsession bullet now drives `Binder.Resume` over the same pinned-pod fixture in both directions: `KeepsRuntime: false` returns `ErrNoIdlePod` with the single drain-request Patch, and `KeepsRuntime: true` creates the SandboxClaim on the `acme`-pinned pod with no Patch — BECAUSE `Resume` reaches `connect` from its own call site (resume.go:121), which is separate from `Claim` (bindclaim.go:56), and only the true case catches a Resume that drops the field (the zero value is false) — ALTERNATIVES: adding only the false case (passes a dropped field); a tier-1 seam on connect's ClaimRequest (adds new surface); asserting a nil Resume error (needs adapter wiring and a workspace base).
